Dataset text · Existing summary · KUs

Q1: How was the training set filtered to reduce homologous leakage from the 100-protein validation set?
  • A: The validation set was chosen randomly after training and contained 669 proteins
  • B: All proteins with sequence identity above 50% were retained, leaving 50,914 training proteins
  • C: HHblits HMM hits with e-value below 0.1 against validation proteins were removed, leaving 34,691 train and 88 validation proteins after length filtering
  • D: Only proteins shorter than 100 residues were kept, with no homology filtering

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The authors use HMM-based screening and later remove sequences longer than 500 residues.

To efficiently validate models during training and minimize possible over-fitting, we separated 100 proteins from the ProteinNet set and compared their Hidden Markov Models generated by HHblits with the Hidden Markov Models of other proteins in the training dataset and validation set using HHblits. Any proteins, which had hits with these 100 validation proteins at an e-value cut-off of less than 0.1, were removed. This left us with the final 39120 proteins for the training set. After removing any proteins with a length more than 500 from both the training and validation sets, the final training and validation sets have 34691 and 88 proteins, respectively.
Q2: What distinguishes the two independent test sets used for homolog-poor and de novo-like evaluation?
  • A: All sets were sampled from the training proteins before 2016
  • B: SPOT-2018 contains 46 proteins and CASP14-FM contains 669 homolog-rich targets
  • C: Neff1-2018 contains 15 proteins with known templates, while CASP14-FM contains 100 validation proteins
  • D: SPOT-2018 contains 669 post-2018 proteins, while Neff1-2018 isolates 46 with Neff=1; CASP14-FM adds 15 free-modeling targets

Gold: D · No context: C · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The benchmark design includes both post-training structures with stringent HMM filtering and subsets specifically lacking homologs or templates.

This led to 669 proteins as a stringent test set named SPOT-2018. To test how predictors perform on de-novo proteins and proteins without homologs, we separated 46 proteins from SPOT-2018 which have Neff=1 forming a test set called Neff1-2018.
Q3: Which ESM-1b attention features entered the six-model ensemble?
  • A: Symmetrized, APC-corrected maps from all 20 heads in the last layer or from 20 heads in every layer
  • B: Only the ESM-1b final embedding vector, without attention maps
  • C: One attention head from the first layer after sequence-profile concatenation
  • D: All 34 transformer layers' raw unsymmetrized attention probabilities

Gold: A · No context: C · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The feature design compares the last layer against all layers, using twenty heads and applying symmetrization plus APC.

Attention maps from ESM-1b were gathered by using all twenty attention heads from the last layer of the transformer as well as twenty attention heads from every layer of the ESM-1b model. For both cases, we symmetrized and applied average product corrections (APC) to the extracted attention maps
Q4: How did the contact definition and sequence-separation categories used for evaluation differ?
  • A: A contact was Cβ distance ≤10 Å; pairs at least 7 residues apart were all long-range
  • B: A contact was Cβ–Cβ distance ≤8 Å; long, medium, and short pairs were separated by ≥24, 12–23, and 7–11 residues
  • C: A contact was Cα distance <4 Å; long, medium, and short were ≥12, 8–11, and 1–7 residues
  • D: A contact was any atom within 8 Å; classes were based on spatial rather than sequence distance

Gold: B · No context: C · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The evaluation follows the stated CASP distance threshold and bins residue pairs by sequence separation.

protein residues are considered to be in contact when there is an inter-residue distance of ≤ 8.0 Å between two C β atoms. A contact between two residues is classified into three types: long (at least 24 residues apart), medium (between 12 to 23 residues apart) and short (between 7-11 residues apart) ranges.
Q5: What network depth and feature combinations defined the final model family?
  • A: Six Transformer-34 models used only MSA profiles and 128 channels
  • B: A 12-layer LSTM used predicted distances but no residue-pair features
  • C: Six ResNet12 models with 64 channels and 3×3 kernels combined last/all-layer ESM attention with optional one-hot and SPOT-1D-Single features
  • D: A single 30-block ResNet used only one-hot inputs and no ESM attention

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The authors trained six models at the maximum depth allowed by GPU memory, with three feature sets and two prediction targets.

In this paper, we use a 12 block ResNet, which is the maximum depth that we could train on the available GPU.
Q6: What was the effect of adding ESM-1b last-layer attention to a one-hot-only baseline on validation performance?
  • A: F1 remained 0.15 and MCC fell to zero
  • B: ROC AUC fell from 0.95 to 0.51 after attention was added
  • C: F1 reached 0.22, a 15% gain over SSCpred
  • D: F1 reached 0.51, with 0.50 MCC and 0.95 ROC AUC, a 242% F1 gain over one-hot input

Gold: D · No context: B · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

ESM attention supplied substantial pairwise signal compared with sequence identity features alone.

The attention maps extracted from the last layer of the ESM-1b lead to 0.51, 0.50, 0.56, and 0.95 for F1, MCC, precision and AUC of ROC, respectively. The result is 242% and 178% improvement in the F1-score over models trained on one-hot encoding and SPOT-1D-Single + one-hot encoding, respectively.
Q7: What did combining all input features add beyond ESM attention from all layers alone?
  • A: It increased F1 by 2.92% and notably improved medium- and long-range precision
  • B: It had no effect on validation performance
  • C: It doubled F1 but reduced all contact precision
  • D: It improved only short-range sensitivity and reduced long-range precision

Gold: A · No context: A · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The combined representation offered a modest F1 gain with particular benefits for medium- and long-range contacts.

As expected, concatenating all features together (one-hot encoding + SPOT-1D-Single + ESM-1B attention maps (all layers)) further showed an increase of 2.92% in F1-score over using the attention maps (all layers) only with noticeable improvement in model precision for medium-and long-range contacts, in particular
Q8: Why were both direct contact classification and distogram prediction retained in the ensemble?
  • A: Distogram prediction was much better and direct prediction failed
  • B: Direct prediction was slightly better, but the difference was small, so both strategies were used
  • C: Direct prediction required MSA, whereas distograms did not
  • D: They produced identical scores by definition and used the same loss

Gold: B · No context: B · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The small performance gap motivated use of both objective types among the six ensemble members.

Table 3 and Supplementary Table S3 shows that direct contact-map prediction performs slightly better, but the difference between the two training strategies is small. Thus, both strategies were employed in different models for our final ensemble.
Q9: How did the ensemble compare with its strongest individual model on the validation set?
  • A: It improved accuracy only by adding evolutionary profiles
  • B: It lowered F1 by 4% while improving only ROC AUC
  • C: Averaging all six models improved F1 by 4% over the second-best individual model
  • D: It matched the best model exactly across all metrics

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The ensemble mean improved across matrices and surpassed every single member in the reported validation comparison.

The performance of the ensemble (SPOT-Contact-Single) is higher than all individual models, with 4% improvement over the second-best individual model for the F1-score.
Q10: What input-length limitation did the use of ESM-1b impose on direct SPOT-Contact-Single predictions?
  • A: It could not predict proteins shorter than 1,024 amino acids
  • B: It supported only proteins with exactly 1,024 residues
  • C: It limited predictions to proteins with at least 2,048 amino acids
  • D: It could not directly predict contact maps for proteins longer than 1,024 amino acids

Gold: D · No context: D · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The authors attribute the upper length limit to the ESM-1b attention-map input.

Using ESM-1b attention map in SPOT-Contact-Single makes it not possible to directly predict contact maps for proteins with more than 1024 amino acids.

run_predprey_model() requires as inputs a vector defining the parameter values (params), a vector of the initial population sizes for the predator and prey species (init), and the time steps over which to run the model (time).

Panel A shows dampened oscillations arising at low prey carrying capacities (K = 200) when the predator (blue) isocline intersects the prey (red) isocline to the right of the "hump"

Tumor PD-L1 selectively suppresses type I interferon in myeloid cells to suppress CTL recruitment to promote lung metastasis

Dataset text · Existing summary · KUs

Q1: What did the tumor–CTL coculture establish about tumor-cell PD-L1 acting directly on cytotoxic T cells?
  • A: With only tumor cells and antigen-specific CTLs present, tPD-L1 conferred no survival advantage to tumor cells
  • B: Tumor PD-L1 directly suppressed CTL cytotoxicity but not activation
  • C: PD-L1 deletion impaired CTL activation only when myeloid cells were absent
  • D: tPD-L1 completely blocked CTL killing whenever tumor cells expressed gp70

Gold: A · No context: C · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The study separates direct tumor–CTL effects from an indirect mechanism requiring the metastatic niche and myeloid cells.

These results indicate that tPD-L1 confers no survival advantage when an antigen-specific effector CTL encounters a tumor cell bearing a cognate peptide-MHC complex, free from any other tumor microenvironmental factors.
Q2: What pattern did tumor-cell PD-L1 knockout produce in primary growth versus lung metastasis?
  • A: Primary tumor growth increased while lung colonization was unchanged
  • B: Primary tumor growth was unchanged, but lung metastasis decreased in a CTL-dependent manner
  • C: Primary tumors shrank, but lung metastases increased independently of CTLs
  • D: Both primary and metastatic growth decreased equally in RAGII-KO mice

Gold: B · No context: B · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The results distinguish a metastatic-site immune-evasion function from a primary-tumor growth effect.

Intriguingly, no difference was observed in primary tumor growth, supporting our in vitro cytotoxicity results (Fig 2A , S2B ). No differences in tumor growth were observed in the WT and PD-L1 KO CT26 tumor at the site of tumor cell injection (Fig. 2E ).
Q3: Which controls argued that tPD-L1 promotes lung metastasis through immune escape rather than initial lung seeding?
  • A: Primary tumors were larger in knockout mice, but lung seeding was not measured
  • B: The effect persisted without adaptive immunity and was linked to extravasation
  • C: Initial gp70 colonization was unchanged at day 1, and WT versus knockout tumors grew equally in RAGII-KO mice
  • D: Knockout cells seeded more strongly at day 1 and grew faster in RAGII-KO mice

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

No initial colonization difference and loss of the genotype effect in adaptive-immune-deficient mice point to CTL-mediated clearance rather than seeding.

No difference in initial tumor cell colonization as measured by tumor cell gp70 level in the lungs was seen one day after tail vein injection (Fig. 3B ), demonstrating that PD-L1 does not enhance DTC colonization efficiency in the lung. Similarly, PD-L1-deficient and competent 4T1 metastasized equally in adaptive-immune deficient congenic RAGII-KO mice (Fig 3C )
Q4: What happened to CD3+ infiltration after tPD-L1 loss, and where was the change observed?
  • A: It did not change in either site
  • B: It increased equally in both sites
  • C: It decreased in lung metastases but increased in the primary tumor
  • D: It increased in lung metastases but not in the primary tumor

Gold: D · No context: C · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The spatially selective change supports a metastatic-niche effect on T-cell recruitment.

Increased infiltration of CD3 + cells after loss of tPD-L1 in lung, but not in the primary tumor were observed (Fig. 3A ), which indicate that tPD-L1 blocked T cell infiltration at the metastatic site.
Q5: What cell population showed transcriptional remodeling in the lung niche after tumor PD-L1 loss, rather than a shift in overall myeloid abundance?
  • A: Myeloid cells showed altered interferon-related state, while bulk myeloid composition did not change
  • B: Myeloid cells disappeared entirely from the lung
  • C: Only tumor cells changed, while leukocytes were unaffected
  • D: CTLs changed transcriptionally, while macrophage abundance doubled

Gold: A · No context: A · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The authors distinguish state changes in myeloid cells from an altered bulk proportion of myeloid cells.

Validating our transcriptomic data, no change in bulk lung myeloid cell composition was observed, though enhanced antigen presentation machinery was detected in macrophages
Q6: How did tumor-cell engagement with macrophage PD-1 alter signaling in RAW264.7 cocultures?
  • A: It reduced SHP-2, increased pSTAT1, and raised MHC-II expression
  • B: It enhanced SHP-2 activation, decreased pSTAT1, and reduced MHC-II expression
  • C: It had no effect unless CTL PD-1 was also engaged
  • D: It increased IFN-β and MHC-II while eliminating PD-1

Gold: B · No context: C · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

These coculture findings connect tPD-L1–mPD-1 engagement to attenuated interferon/STAT1 signaling and antigen presentation.

Engagement with 4T1 tPD-L1 enhanced SHP-2 activation and decreased pSTAT1 levels in RAW cells, diminishing expression of MHC class II both on 4T1 and RAW cells
Q7: What result supports a negative-feedback relationship between PD-1/PD-L1 and type I interferon in the coculture system?
  • A: PD-1 loss reduced sensitivity to poly(I:C) and lowered interferon
  • B: IFN-β had no effect on checkpoint molecules
  • C: Disrupting RAW PD-1 or 4T1 PD-L1 enhanced IFN-β production, while IFN-β can induce PD-L1/L2
  • D: Disrupting either receptor abolished IFN-β, which then suppressed PD-L1

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The axis is induced by interferon yet feeds back to attenuate interferon signaling.

Accordingly, disruption of RAW PD-1 expression or 4T1 PD-L1 expression enhanced IFN production (Fig. 6J ). Given the identity of IFN as a potent PD-L1/L2 inducer 24 , these findings demonstrate the existence of a negative feedback circuit in which the PD-1/L1 axis is induced by and then attenuates type I interferon signaling.

Q8: Why did the authors caution that similar angiographic results did not establish equivalence?

Incomplete, symptom/discretion-driven imaging and small sample size meant the analysis lacked strong power to rule out angiographic differences.

Second, follow-up coronary angiography was not routinely performed in all patients. We decided follow-up angiography mainly depends on ischemic symptoms or by physician's discretion regardless of patient's symptoms. Well developed collaterals and myocardial viability could influence the ischemic symptoms. So we could not avoid some confounders. Third, the sample size was relatively small, and although we could not find the differences in angiographic outcomes between two groups, the study did not have strong power to prove a difference in angiographic events.

Q1: How did the CATR affinity measured by the two assays on DPC-refolded AAC3 compare, and what did the authors emphasize about the ITC value?

  • C: ITC gave ~15 µM and NMR ~150 µM, both far weaker than membrane AAC3; the ITC value was especially challenged by the nanomolar consensus.

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The paper reports an approximately tenfold assay difference and notes that both micromolar estimates are orders of magnitude weaker than membrane binding values.

To validate the integrity of their refolded protein, the authors first used ITC to measure a Kd of ~15 µM for the binding of CATR to DPC--refolded AAC3. They then determined CATR--dependent chemical shift perturbations by NMR, giving a Kd of ~150 µM.
Q2: What corrected literature value undermined the comparison used for ATR binding in the work being criticized?
  • A: A 72 nM value was corrected to 192 nM.
  • B: A reported 192 nM value was corrected to 192 µM.
  • C: A 15 µM CATR value was corrected to 1.2 nM.
  • D: A reported 192 µM ATR Kd was corrected in an erratum to 192 nM.

Gold: D · No context: A · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The authors identify a typographical unit error in the cited Babot et al. value: micromolar should have been nanomolar.

Yet, the authors only compare their values to a Kd of 192 µM for the binding of ATR taken from one particular study by Babot et al., 2012 6 . Importantly, the µM unit used in this reference was a typographical error and the Kd value has recently been corrected to 192 nM in an erratum 7 .
Q3: What comparison did the authors use to show that native AAC3 transport is strongly inhibited by CATR?
  • A: ADP transport was half-maximally inhibited at 1.2 nM CATR.
  • B: ADP binding had a Kd of 1.2 mM in membrane.
  • C: ATP transport was fully inhibited at 72 nM CATR.
  • D: ADP transport was half-maximally inhibited at 1.2 µM CATR.

Gold: A · No context: C · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The cited transport result is a nanomolar inhibition concentration and supports the claimed strong physiological-affinity range.

In fact, published Kd values for CATR binding to AAC in the membrane are all in the low nanomolar range (Supplementary Table 1 ), consistent with our own measurements that show that ADP transport by AAC3 is half--maximally inhibited by 1.2 nM CATR (Fig. 1a ).
Q4: What was the mean CATR dissociation constant measured by ITC for native yeast AAC3 purified in maltoside/cardiolipin, and how did the authors qualify the experiment?
  • A: 150 nM; two of ten trials succeeded due to high enthalpy.
  • B: 72 nM; only 2 of 10 trials succeeded because the enthalpic change was low and apo-state unstable.
  • C: 15 nM; only one of ten trials succeeded because CATR dissociated rapidly.
  • D: 72 µM; all ten trials succeeded easily.

Gold: B · No context: B · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The paper gives an average of 72 nM and explicitly cautions that the measurement was technically difficult, succeeding in only two trials.

Our ITC measurements using native AAC3 from yeast mitochondria purified in dodecyl-maltoside/tetraoleoyl cardiolipin gave an average Kd of 72 nM (Fig. 1b ), which is 200 to 2000--fold lower than those reported in Brüschweiler et al.. These experiments were very difficult to do (only 2 out of 10 trials succeeded), as the enthalpic change is low and the apo--state is very unstable in detergent 5 .
Q5: According to the proposed structural explanation, what stabilizes the unliganded carrier’s transmembrane helix bundle in its native setting?
  • A: A dense network of inter-domain polar side-chain bonds on the cytoplasmic face.
  • B: DPC micelles that mimic a bilayer’s lateral pressure.
  • C: Lateral membrane pressure opposed by counter-pressure from the water-filled cavity.
  • D: Permanent covalent links between the helices and CATR.

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: C

The authors say the helix ring is held together largely by membrane lateral pressure and counter-pressure from the internal cavity, explaining detergent vulnerability.

There are very few polar side chain interactions in AAC3 that stabilize the structure and they are mainly found on the matrix side, where they form intra--domain rather than inter--domain interactions 3 . The ring of transmembrane a-helices is held together largely by the lateral pressure of the membrane and counter pressure from the water--filled cavity (Supplementary Fig. 1a ) 3 , explaining why unliganded AAC3 in detergent micelles is prone to unfolding.
Q6: What happened in the CPM thermostability assay when native AAC3 was diluted into 3 mM DPC, and what did added CATR do?
  • A: The melt transition moved lower with CATR, indicating tight binding in DPC.
  • B: A typical melt curve appeared and CATR shifted it upward, proving native folding.
  • C: No fluorescence was detected until CATR was added, indicating refolding.
  • D: The signal was high without a melt transition, and CATR had no effect, consistent with loss of a functional binding site.

Gold: D · No context: C · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

Unlike maltoside, DPC produced a high signal with no transition and CATR did not shift it, which the authors interpret as non-native AAC3 lacking functional binding.

When the same AAC3 preparation was diluted into 3 mM DPC, a high signal was observed with no transition, showing that AAC3 in DPC is in a non--native state (Fig. 1d ). In this case, CATR addition had no effect, demonstrating that there was no functional binding site.
Q7: What functional consequence followed dilution of AAC3 into DPC before liposome reconstitution?
  • A: A complete loss of CATR-sensitive ADP uptake, unlike the maltoside-diluted control.
  • B: A twofold increase in CATR-sensitive ADP uptake.
  • C: No uptake in either DPC or maltoside controls.
  • D: CATR-insensitive uptake that recovered after 48 hours.

Gold: A · No context: A · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The uptake comparison is a direct functional test: DPC pretreatment abolished the inhibitor-sensitive transport seen in the maltoside control.

Consistent with these findings, dilution of AAC3 into DPC before reconstitution resulted in a complete loss of CATR--sensitive ADP uptake in liposomes, in contrast to control tests where the protein was diluted into dodecyl--maltoside prior to reconstitution (Supplementary Fig. 2 ).
Q8: Why do the authors argue that CATR binding itself stabilizes AAC3 in detergent?
  • A: CATR removes the transmembrane helices from the membrane and exposes them to water.
  • B: CATR makes numerous polar and van der Waals contacts that cross-link most transmembrane helices.
  • C: CATR covalently attaches AAC3 to cardiolipin.
  • D: CATR raises the protein’s isoelectric point, neutralizing membrane destabilization.

Gold: B · No context: C · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The proposed mechanism is ligand-mediated helix cross-linking through many interactions, producing both high affinity and increased detergent stability.

Binding of CATR introduces a large number of polar and van der Waals interactions, which cross--link most of the transmembrane α--helices of AAC3 together 3, 17 , explaining the high affinity of CATR for AAC3 as well as the improved stability in detergents
Q9: What discrepancy did the authors identify for ADP binding measured by NMR on refolded AAC3?
  • A: The 500 nM value was about 85-fold below membrane consensus.
  • B: The 150 μM value was 85-fold above membrane consensus and 25-fold below the native protein.
  • C: The 500 μM value was about 85-fold above membrane consensus and 25-fold above the solubilized-carrier value.
  • D: The 500 μM value matched the solubilized carrier but was 25-fold below membrane consensus.

Gold: C · No context: C · Dataset paper text: C · Existing Gemini summary: C · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The paper explicitly quantifies the difference of the NMR-derived 500 μM ADP Kd relative to membrane and solubilized reference values.

We also note that the Kd of ADP binding determined by NMR (500 μM) 1 is ~85--fold higher than the published consensus values of the carrier in the mitochondrial membrane and ~25--fold higher than for the solubilized carrier (Supplementary Table 2 ).
Q10: Which caveat about interpreting NMR chemical-shift perturbations did the authors raise given AAC3’s charge and the ligands’ charge?
  • A: The shifts cannot arise nonspecifically because ADP and CATR are neutral at physiological pH.
  • B: The shifts must reflect covalent ligand attachment because AAC3 is negatively charged.
  • C: The shifts establish binding at the central cavity despite all assigned residues being elsewhere.
  • D: At high ligand concentrations and temperatures, shifts could reflect nonspecific electrostatic interactions with positively charged AAC3.

Gold: D · No context: D · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

AAC3 has pI 9.82 and is highly positive, whereas CATR, ADP and ATP are negative at neutral pH; the authors warn that concentrated ligand could cause nonspecific interactions.

AAC3 has an isoelectric point of 9.82, meaning that it is highly positively charged, whereas CATR and the substrates ADP and ATP are negatively charged molecules at neutral pH. The chemical shift perturbations could represent non--specific interactions of CATR or ADP with AAC3 promoted by the high concentrations and temperatures used in these NMR experiments.

The control baseline was substantially higher than the rates under the most toxic nanoparticle exposures.

Brine shrimp normally showed a hatching rate of 74%+5.5, the viability of these hatched nauplii was about 97%.
Q3: At 100 mg/L, what hatching outcome was reported for Ag and CuO nanoparticles?
  • A: Hatching fell to about 29%, and only about half of hatched nauplii were motile/viable.
  • B: Hatching remained near 74%, with 97% viability.

  • D: Hatching was 60%, with 85% viability.

Silver and copper oxide produced the strongest hatching effects; viability among hatchlings was also reduced at the high concentration.

The highest effect was seen in presence of silver and copper oxide nanoparticles. The hatching rate dropped to about 29% in the two nanoparticles; of these only 50% were motile and viable at a concentration of 100mg/L
Q4: What was the 24-hour LC50 reported for silver nanoparticles in the larval assay?
  • A: Around 100 mg/L.
  • B: Around 10.12 mg/L.
  • C: Around 0.1012 mg/L.
  • D: Around 1.012 mg/L.

Gold: B · No context: C · Dataset paper text: B · Existing Gemini summary: B · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: B

The article reports a 24-hour LC50 near 10.12 mg/L for silver nanoparticles.

The nauplii showed variability in viability to various nanoparticles. The most toxic was the silver nanoparticles and silver in ionic form (Table 2 ). Silver in ionic form was highly toxic as 80% of the nauplii were dead in 24 hours in silver nitrate solution (Figure 4 ). The LC 50 of the silver nanoparticles was around 10.12mg/L at 24hours.

  • A: Silver nitrate killed 8% of nauplii, less than controls.
  • B: Silver nitrate improved hatching to 80%.
  • C: Silver nitrate killed 80% of nauplii within 24 h, indicating strong toxicity of ionic silver as well.
  • D: Silver nitrate caused no mortality, showing only particles were toxic.

The soluble ionic form was itself highly toxic, so the authors did not attribute all observed silver effects solely to nanoparticle form.

Silver in ionic form was highly toxic as 80% of the nauplii were dead in 24 hours in silver nitrate solution
Q6: What was the reported correlation between hatching-rate effects and larval-mortality test results?
  • A: A strong positive correlation with coefficient +0.7853.
  • B: A perfect inverse correlation of −1.0.
  • C: No correlation, coefficient 0.07853.
  • D: A strong inverse correlation with coefficient −0.7853.

Gold: D · No context: C · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

Lower hatching was associated with greater mortality in the separate larval test, producing the reported negative correlation.

On comparing the two methods, we found a very good inverse correlation between the results obtained on effects of NPs on hatching rates and mortality test on the hatched larvae. The particles that reduced the hatching rate showed an increase in mortality in the ARC test using the normally hatched nauplii. The correlation test showed a coefficient of -0.7853.

  • A: P=0.683, with no statistically significant difference in mean values.
  • B: P=0.05, exactly at the significance boundary.
  • C: P=0.683, demonstrating a significant difference.
  • D: P=0.006, demonstrating a significant difference.

Gold: A · No context: A · Dataset paper text: A · Existing Gemini summary: A · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: A

The paired comparison did not reveal a significant difference in mean values for the paired assay measures.

Comparing the viability of nauplii (inverse of mortality) in ARC test with the hatching rate showed no statistically significant difference in the mean values for each concentration of different particles (P=0.683) in the paired T test.

In the ARC test; we do see sedimentation and flocculation of nanoparticles by 48 hrs.
Q10: How did the authors frame gut accumulation as a possible mechanism, and what alternative evidence suggested oxidative stress?
  • A: All nanoparticle mortality was proven to be oxidative and no gut uptake occurred.
  • B: Gut accumulation protected nauplii, and ROS staining occurred only in controls.
  • C: Particles never entered the gut; mortality was exclusively due to starvation.
  • D: Gut clogging could contribute to death, while ROS/superoxide staining—especially for silver—suggested oxidative stress for some exposures.

Gold: D · No context: D · Dataset paper text: D · Existing Gemini summary: D · Qwen27B Knowledge Units: D

The mechanism is proposed rather than conclusively proven: particles were seen in digestive tracts, and reactive oxygen staining was strongest for silver, with several other materials showing gut accumulation without staining.

The mortality observed may be due to the uptake of the eNPs clogging the gut as most of the nauplii in presence of eNPS show presence of particles in the digestive tract. In the hatching test, the dead Artemia and partially hatched cysts show increased stress in presence of nanoparticles as indicated by DCDFA staining. Even the nauplii exposed to some nanoparticles do show accumulation of ROS and superoxide especially with silver nanoparticles. But with remaining nanoparticles it must be the NP clogging in the gut that could cause mortality.
